# Daily strategy inbox (accumulator)
<!-- word_count: 32923 -->

**Purpose:** **Append-only** scratch surface for the **current local calendar day** while you run **`strategy`**, read briefs, or capture links. Polished prose is **not** required â€” bullets, paste, URLs, half-sentences.

**Accumulator date:** The **`YYYY-MM-DD`** on the **`Accumulator for`** line is the **local calendar date implied by the system timestamp** (host clock when the file is edited, or the session **Today's date** field in the assistant environmentâ€”same source of truth). **Do not** set or hold that date by **weave** alone; **update** it when the **actual calendar day** changes.

**Footgun (why this drifts to â€œtomorrowâ€):** **`Accumulator for:` must always be *today* (operator local day)** â€” not the date in a **daily-brief-YYYY-MM-DD** filename you just edited, not a **Grok â€œWatchlist for Tomorrowâ€** line, and **not** a deferred batch label like **`Author X â€¦ 2026-04-18`** (those are **subsection grep keys** and may point at a *future* calendar day on purpose; see [STATUS.md](STATUS.md) / `days.md` weave notes). If the top line shows **today + 1**, roll it back to **today** and keep future-dated work in **row text** only.

**Verify (optional):** `python3 scripts/verify_strategy_inbox_accumulator.py` â€” exits **0** only if `Accumulator for` matches **today** on the machine; use `--date YYYY-MM-DD` to assert the **operator** calendar day when the runnerâ€™s clock differs (e.g. UTC CI). Cursor loads [`.cursor/rules/strategy-inbox-accumulator-date.mdc`](../../../../.cursor/rules/strategy-inbox-accumulator-date.mdc) when this file is open.

**`Accumulator` / inbox subsection dates â‰  `days.md` headings (anti-split):** The accumulator line and labels like **`### Author X / YT ingest â€” YYYY-MM-DD`** are **scratch organization and grep keys**. They **do not** require a matching **`## YYYY-MM-DD`** in [`chapters/YYYY-MM/days.md`](chapters/2026/2026-04/days.md) â€” especially when the operator has stated a **last-ingest boundary** or when assistant work continues across local midnights. **Weave** into the **operator-chosen notebook day** (or a single episodic heading); add a **weave date note** if publication dates and heading dates diverge. Full contract: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md Â§ `days.md` date keys â€” semantics and anti-split](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md#days-md-date-semantics).

**`grounding_delta` (optional):** One short line per day (or per heavy-ingest burst) naming **what changed** in MEM / relevance grounding for **this** notebook day â€” e.g. new `MEMâ€“RELEVANCEâ€“*` hook, SCHOLAR relay, or an explicit â€œno new groundingâ€ stamp. **Purpose:** keep digest-heavy sessions **tied** to the repoâ€™s grounding pipeline without merging judgment into the Record. Place it **immediately under** the **`Accumulator for:`** line (or inside the same-day scratch block) so greps and weaves see it beside fresh ingests.

**EOD strategy rhythm (default):** **One end-of-day strategy session** per local calendar day composes **`days.md`** + **`strategy-page`** blocks from **this file**, **[`../source-archive/statecraft/`](../source-archive/statecraft/README.md)** (primary bulk verbatim), briefs, and post-**`thread`** author corpus â€” not on ingest alone and **not** continuously intra-day unless **breaking glass**. The **timestamp** / operator boundary still determines **which** `## YYYY-MM-DD` block receives that session (often beside **`dream`** or the first pass after local midnight); the **`Accumulator for:`** line tracks the **scratch day**, not the notebook heading by itself â€” [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md) Â§ *End-of-day strategy session*, Â§ *Compose choice and section weighting*, Â§ *`days.md` date keys*. **Optional:** log compose passes for learning â€” [FOLD-LEARNING.md](FOLD-LEARNING.md) (`scripts/log_strategy_fold.py`). *Legacy Â§ title:* â€œWeave rhythm.â€

**Thread rhythm (author corpus):** After **`thread:<expert_id>`** lines are in this file, operator **`thread`** rebuilds **`strategy-expert-<expert_id>.md`** rolling blocks only â€” **`python3 scripts/strategy_thread.py`** (same flags as `scripts/strategy_expert_corpus.py`). **Not** **EOD compose**: does **not** touch **`days.md`**, **`strategy-page`** blocks, or **`Accumulator for`**. Spec: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md) Â§ *Thread (terminology)*.

**Third-party scan / X-only hygiene:** Do **not** paste assistant or digest **paraphrases** (e.g. daily scan summaries without URLs) into **`experts/<expert_id>/transcript.md`**. For claims that need a named author lane, add **paste-ready lines here first** with a **URL** and **`thread:<expert_id>`** when cold attributes speech to that author; if no primary yet, use a one-line **stub** with **`verify:pending-primary`** â€” verbatim transcripts stay for **actual ingested speech**, not second-hand scan text.

**Inbox â†’ source archive (order of operations):** The **inbox** is the **registry** (stubs, **`thread:`**, links, **`verify:`**). **Durable** full text belongs under **[`../source-archive/statecraft/YYYY-MM-DD/`](../source-archive/statecraft/README.md)** as soon as the capture is **indexed** here (one-line stub + path, or the same run as an ingest script that writes the file e.g. **`python3 scripts/fetch_strategy_raw_input.py --apply`**, which also appends a matching stub). Do **not** treat the source archive as a second capture lane you use **instead of** the inbox. Full policy: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md) Â§ *Split ingest model*.

**Post ingest cadence:** Aim for **at least five** strategy ingests from Post per local day (claim â†’ why it matters â†’ URL, plus verify tags as needed). **Five is a floor, not a cap** â€” capturing **more than five** on busy days is **normal**, not exceptional. Same one-line shape scales to 6+ rows without a separate workflow.

**Post and exact URLs:** **Do not** require a **per-post** permalink for a line to count. Default **`https://x.com/<handle>`** (profile) is enough to anchor attribution. Use **`verify:`** tails like **`X-account+approx-date+optional-pin`** â€” add a **status URL** when the operator has it; **EOD / Links** promotion can still **`optional-pin`** later. Ingests are **not** blocked for lacking **`pin-exact-status-URL`**.

### Paste-ready one-liner (canonical unit)

**Purpose:** One **grep-friendly** line per ingest (clipboard-safe, easy to append in bulk).

**Suggested shape** (example, not a strict schema): optional source token (`X`, `YT`, etc.) **|** short **gist** (claim + why it matters) **|** URL (**X:** profile URL OK if no status link), with an optional `verify:` tail for epistemic flags (e.g. `verify:OSINT-unverified`, `verify:X-account+optional-pin`).

**Commentator threads (stable ids):** For recurring authors and **`batch-analysis`** pairings, see [strategy-commentator-threads.md](strategy-commentator-threads.md) â€” optional **`thread:<expert_id>`** in the **`verify:`** tail **only** when **cold** attributes speech/analysis to that **named** author (e.g. `verify:â€¦ | thread:davis`). **Wires** without a named author speaker â†’ **`verify:wire-RSS`** (and topic tags), **no** author **`thread:`**. **Crossing rules** (what may mix across threads): **Crossing filters** section in that file; optional tails **`membrane:single`**, **`membrane:pair`**, **`crosses:<id>+<id>`**, **`seam:<slug>+<slug>`** (often on **`batch-analysis`** when **`crosses:`** is not two **`expert_id`**s). **Recommended one-liners** (e.g. **Pape** vs **Barnes** domestic plane): **Distinctive lane shorthands** in that same file. When you use **`thread:`**, you may rebuild the per-author rolling corpus: **`python3 scripts/strategy_thread.py`** (operator **`thread`**; delegates to `strategy_expert_corpus.py`) â†’ **`strategy-expert-<expert_id>.md`** in this directory (last **7** days inside the script block; **not** Record). See [strategy-commentator-threads.md](strategy-commentator-threads.md) and [expert-ingest-corpus/README.md](expert-ingest-corpus/README.md) (redirect).

**No persistent thread (normal):** If the ingest is for **that sessionâ€™s** analysis only â€” not a voice you plan to **track** week-to-week â€” **leave off** **`thread:`**. Keep **cold**, **hook**, **URL**, **`verify:`**, and **topic** tags (`IRAN`, `ROME`, â€¦). Add **`membrane:single`** when you want grep to show **â€œdonâ€™t auto-merge this into multi-thread batch claims by default.â€** Full wording: **Ephemeral / one-shot ingests** in [strategy-commentator-threads.md](strategy-commentator-threads.md).

**Optional grep tags (primary threads):** When an ingest is part of the **Rome** thread, you may prefix the **cold** clause with **`ROME`** or **`LeoXIV`**. When it is part of the **JD Vance / VP** thread, use **`JDVance`** or **`VANCE`**. When it is part of the **Putin / Kremlin** thread, use **`PUTIN`** or **`KREMLIN`**. When it is part of the **PRC / Beijing** thread, use **`PRC`**, **`CN`**, or **`CHINA`**. When it is part of the **IRI / Tehran** thread, use **`IRAN`**, **`IRI`**, or **`TEHRAN`** (e.g. `cold: IRI | MFA statement â€” â€¦`). When an ingest flags **narrative escalation** (register shift from empirical / wire-tractable claims toward moralized or metaphysical framesâ€”e.g. spiritual-warfare language on a political stillâ€”see [.cursor/skills/skill-strategy/SKILL.md](../docs/skill-work/work-strategy/SKILL-STRATEGY-DEPRECATED.md) Â§ *Narrative escalation*), prefix the **cold** clause with **`narrative-escalation`**. **Retroactive spine (Trump â†” Christianity / papacy / religion, 2016â†’):** [trump-religion-papacy-arc.md](trump-religion-papacy-arc.md). Example: `rg 'IRAN|IRI|TEHRAN|PRC|CN|CHINA|PUTIN|KREMLIN|JDVance|VANCE|ROME|LeoXIV|narrative-escalation'`.

#### Arc lens tags

Optional arc lens tags route Ukraine/Europe inputs into WORK-layer continuity lenses. Use them only when a source materially updates the lens; they are grep handles, not truth labels, verification status, or Record facts.

- `arc:peace-leverage` - terms available as battlefield, industrial, fiscal, or political facts accumulate.
- `arc:capacity-gap` - gap between Western strategic language and execution capacity.
- `arc:proxy-patron` - patron/client burden transfer, exit management, or client exposure.

Place arc tags near the hook or topic tags in a normal one-line inbox entry. A single item may carry multiple arc tags only when it genuinely updates multiple arcs. Do not retroactively tag old rows unless the operator explicitly asks for a backfill pass.

#### Optional two-tier gist (cold claim // operator hook)

**Problem:** A single **gist** that mixes **what the source did** with **why it matters for this notebook day** can smuggle **Judgment** into the inbox and blur the boundary with `days.md`.

**Convention (optional, still one line):** Split the middle field with **` // `** (space-double-slash-space):

1. **Cold** â€” minimal, attribution-safe paraphrase: who said what, what artifact (post, wire, video), **without** folding in notebook-specific framing.
2. **Hook** â€” one short clause: **why this line exists today** (tie to brief Â§, Judgment fork, batch-analysis theme, or â€œfiling for verifyâ€).

**Pattern:**
`source-token | cold: <clause> // hook: <clause> | URL | verify:â€¦`

**When to use:** Busy news days, nested QTs, or any ingest where you want **grep** to separate **source fact** from **operator placement**. When the ingest is trivial or cold/hook would duplicate each other, keep a **single** gist (original shape).

**Examples (illustrative):**

- `X | cold: @barnes_law quote-tweet Disclose.tv summarizing executive TS post on Hormuz blockade + toll interdiction // hook: third domestic pole vs op-ed â€œcardâ€ vs spiral satire; pin status URL | https://x.com/barnes_law | verify:â€¦`
- `wire | cold: Reuters Islamabad talks pause, disagreements remain // hook: aligns Â§1e window; stack with Tasnim fa | https://â€¦ | verify:â€¦`

**Live demo (scratch):** Under the append line for **2026-04-12**, the **Parsi**, **JTN/commentariat domestic fork** (historical X lines, not indexed as `hormuz-story-fork` after **2026-04-14**), and **Barnes** ingests are refactored to **cold // hook** as in-repo pattern examples.

**Assistant default:** Offer **cold // hook** when the operatorâ€™s capture is **Judgment-sensitive** or **multi-chain**; otherwise **single gist** is fine.

### Long-form verbatim (`thread:`)

When you need **substantial quoted speech** (interview, monologue segment), put **`thread:<expert_id>`** on the **first** paste-ready line, then continue the quote on **following lines** without a leading `- ` at column 0 (plain paragraphs and blank lines are included until the next top-level `- ` bullet or `##` heading). **Budget:** **â‰¤ ~2000 words** per block (triage warns above that); **7-day** pruning keeps each **`-transcript.md`** near a **â‰¤ ~20k word** soft file cap. Run **`python3 scripts/strategy_thread.py`** (operator **`thread`**) to triage inbox â†’ per-author **`-transcript.md`**.

**Full transcript / complete input capture (no trim):** Store the **entire** body under **[`../source-archive/statecraft/YYYY-MM-DD/<slug>.md`](../source-archive/statecraft/README.md)** (folder = **`pub_date`** when known). Keep **this file** to a **one-line stub** plus **`verify:`** pointer to that path â€” do **not** duplicate megabyte pastes in the inbox scratch. Contract: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md Â§ Split ingest model](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md#split-ingest-model).

### Multi-item ingest (optional common analysis)

When the operator captures **two or more** excerpts in one pass, **items stay separate** â€” still **one canonical line per excerpt** (separate grep targets; separate Links when the inbox weaves into `days.md`). **Same interview URL for two experts:** repeat the URL on **two lines**, each with its own **`thread:<id>`**; see **Same transcript, show, or panel** in [strategy-commentator-threads.md](strategy-commentator-threads.md). **Default:** assistant **drafts** the bundle in chat; **append only after operator approval** (or **`EXECUTE`**).

**Optional:** add **one** short **common analysis** immediately **after** the ingests it covers (not a third ingest). **Placement:** the `batch-analysis` line **immediately follows** the **last** ingest in the set â€” order is the membership anchor (no separate `paired-with` field; the line must **stand alone** when read in isolation). Use it to name **tension**, **comparison**, or **optional weak convergence** across the batch so **`dream`** can weave one **Judgment** without duplicating long synthesis.

**Lightweight forms** (pick one):

- **Single metadata line:**
  `batch-analysis | YYYY-MM-DD | <short theme> | <1â€“2 clauses: tension-first; optional *weak* convergence if clearly labeled>`

- **Mini-block** (slightly more room, still bounded):

```text
--- batch-analysis | YYYY-MM-DD | <short label> ---
<2â€“5 sentences: relationship between items; divergence worth tracking; optional â€œtest nextâ€>
--- end batch-analysis ---
```

**Guardrails:** Keep batch analysis **about one screen max**; skip it when items are unrelated or when a single line per item is enough.

**Default assistant behavior (ingest lines):** When the operator asks for **strategy ingest** in Cursor, the assistantâ€™s **default on-disk target** for those lines is **below the append line in this file** â€” not `session-transcript.md` unless the operator asks for a **session audit trail** (see [work-menu-conventions â€” Auditing picks](../work-menu-conventions.md#6-auditing-picks-choice-journal)).

**Default assistant behavior (`batch-analysis`):** Prefer **proposing** a draft **`batch-analysis` line in chat** (tension-first; optional weak convergence) for operator **copy or reject**; **append** to this file only on operator request (**EXECUTE** / explicit paste). Do **not** treat batch as a substitute for each ingestâ€™s **`verify:`** tail. Full contract: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md) Â§ *Daily strategy inbox*.

**Weave:** Inbox scratch merges into **`chapters/YYYY-MM/days.md`** as one **`## YYYY-MM-DD`** block (Chronicle / Reflection / References / Foresight â€” synthesize, not a raw dump) when **`dream`** runs (**day-end**, timestamp-aligned), when the operator **manually** directs a weave (**intra-day cadence**), or on equivalent explicit instruction. **Assistants:** do **not** merge into `days.md` on strategy-ingest alone; keep captures **here** until **`dream`**, **`weave`**, **`weave`**, or explicit instruction. Full rules: [STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md](STRATEGY-NOTEBOOK-ARCHITECTURE.md) Â§ *Daily strategy inbox*; **`dream`**: [.cursor/skills/dream/SKILL.md](../../../../.cursor/skills/dream/SKILL.md).

**Length (scratch section only â€” below the append line):** When the scratch body exceeds **~20000 characters**, **prune from the top** (oldest lines first) in **~5000-character blocks** until **â‰¤ ~20000 characters** remain (repeat if a single paste still leaves you above the limit). Re-count after large pastes. Optional: full clear to the template below anytime.

**Git:** Prior versions remain in history when you commit.

Title: HORMUZ OPENING, CEASEFIRE ENDING: Conflicting Messages
Guests: Lt. Col. Daniel Davis & Larry Johnson

Daniel Davis: President Trump has announced that the Strait of Hormuz is open. That's great news. Hopefully this means the war is coming to an end â€” hopefully by next Wednesday when the current ceasefire period ends.
The Iranian Foreign Minister, Abbas Araghchi, also said yes, the Strait is open, but it is contingent upon the ceasefire in Lebanon. So we're good to go.
Now there are real problems with that, because what does it actually mean for the Strait to be open? What does the Iranian side say it means versus what President Trump says it means? And even before the ink dries on these social media posts, weâ€™ve already got contradictions.
Larry, let me properly introduce you: Larry Johnson, former CIA analyst, runs Son of the New American Revolution, and a great friend of the show.
What was the first thing you thought when you heard that both President Trump and the Iranian side announced â€” within minutes of each other â€” that the Strait of Hormuz was open?

Larry Johnson: Theyâ€™re not paying attention to what the U.S. government is actually saying. Iâ€™ve been talking to folks in the military and theyâ€™re going, â€œWTF, what is going on?â€
You say the Strait of Hormuz is open, right? But then Trump says the naval blockade is still in full effect. So if the blockade is still in effect, then the Strait is not really open.

Daniel Davis: Let me show you what President Trump posted this morning at 9:27:
â€œThe Strait of Hormuz is completely open and ready for business and full passage. But the naval blockade â€” the American naval blockade â€” will remain in full force and effect as it pertains only to Iran.â€
Then the Iranian Foreign Ministry spokesman posted something similar to what Araghchi said: this is contingent upon the implementation of certain terms and conditions for the ceasefire in Lebanon.
And hereâ€™s the rub: if the naval blockade continues, it will be considered a violation of the ceasefire and passage through the Strait of Hormuz will be closed again.
He also laid out three conditions:

Ships must be commercial â€” passage of military ships is prohibited, and ships/cargo cannot be linked to belligerent states.
Ships must pass through routes designated by Iran (meaning Iran retains control).
Ship passage must be coordinated with Iranian forces responsible for it.

So Trump says the Strait is fully open â€” except itâ€™s only open for what we want to come out, while Iran stays blocked. Iran is saying itâ€™s either all open or none of it is open.
What does that mean going forward?

Larry Johnson: Iran is the only one in a position to keep it closed. The United States canâ€™t open it. I was watching Gordon Chang and that other guy, Quinn, drawing parallels to the Malacca Strait. Thatâ€™s nonsense. Iran has shore-based cruise missiles, ballistic missiles, underwater drones, surface drones, and aerial drones. They can close the Strait whenever they want without even putting ships out there. You donâ€™t have that capability in the Malacca Strait.
I thought we might actually be on the verge of an exit ramp after the Hezbollah-Israel ceasefire. Then I saw what Scott Bessent (Treasury Secretary) did yesterday â€” they reimposed sanctions on Iran the very same day they announced a ceasefire.
Four weeks ago they lifted sanctions on Iranian oil and on Russia to restore market stability. Now they double down on sanctions. One of Iranâ€™s 10-point demands is that all sanctions must be lifted â€” and itâ€™s not negotiable.
Is there nobody in the Trump administration who understands how contradictory these messages are? Iran is not going to surrender on that point.
On top of that, the talks in Islamabad happened largely because of Chinese influence with Pakistan â€” and Bessent threatens China. The Chinese are pissed off. I think theyâ€™ve reached their limit. Thereâ€™s not going to be a meeting between Xi Jinping and Donald Trump.

Daniel Davis: Trump also posted: â€œIran has agreed to never close the Strait of Hormuz again. It will no longer be used as a weapon against the world.â€ Then he thanked Pakistan, said the deal is not tied to Lebanon, claimed Iran with the help of the USA is removing all sea mines (thereâ€™s no evidence of that), and said NATO offered help but he told them to stay away because theyâ€™re a paper tiger.
Another post said the USA will get all the â€œnuclear dustâ€ created by our B2 bombers, and no money will exchange hands in any way.
This morning there were reports of a possible deal to unfreeze $20 billion of Iranian assets in exchange for this â€œnuclear dust.â€ What do you make of all this? How is any of this supposed to work? Is there any truth to it?

Larry Johnson: Donald Trump is detached from reality. He is living in a fantasy world and none of the people around him are willing to tell him the truth.
Just because he writes something on Truth Social doesnâ€™t make it true. He is delusional. If your mother or elderly parent was acting like this, you wouldnâ€™t let them drive. This guy is capable of starting a nuclear war.
The Strait of Hormuz is not â€œwide open.â€ It is under Iranian control. We have no control over it. Our blockade is miles offshore. Youâ€™ve seen the photos of Marines on the ships â€” theyâ€™re not even getting full rations. When 20â€“23-year-old Marines arenâ€™t being fed properly, youâ€™ve got serious problems.
This is a total failure of leadership. Officers are supposed to eat last. Instead, it looks like the officers eat first. Thatâ€™s not how you treat troops you expect to put their lives on the line.
Trump has zero empathy. Heâ€™s divorced from reality. The American people need to stand up. He must be removed from office as soon as possible. This is dangerous.

Daniel Davis: As I see it, there are three main options for how this war could end.
Option 1: Trump agrees to base a diplomatic agreement on Iranâ€™s 10-point plan. At minimum that would mean lifting sanctions, providing security guarantees, some form of reparations, and limited uranium enrichment/reprocessing (Iran sees even limited enrichment as an act of sovereignty).
This would be the best outcome for the world and actually has a chance of working. What do you think?

Larry Johnson: That would be the ideal outcome, but based on what weâ€™ve seen in the last 24 hours â€” doubling down on sanctions and maintaining the blockade while claiming the Strait is open â€” that option is now off the table.

Daniel Davis: Option 2: Trump doubles down. He listens to people like Jack Keane who say â€œgive nothing, take everything.â€ He launches a massive air campaign to try to crush Iran once and for all â€” obliterating energy infrastructure, bridges, and the economy â€” hoping to force total submission.

Larry Johnson: Unfortunately, I think thatâ€™s where this is headed. Within Trumpâ€™s delusional mindset, he believes the U.S. is winning militarily and just needs to finish the job. But the United States cannot actually destroy Iran. We have deluded ourselves about our military potency. We donâ€™t have that capability anymore.

Daniel Davis: Option 3: Trump recognizes the limits of power and plays the long game â€” ramping up sanctions (â€œOperation Economic Furyâ€) and trying to outlast Iran economically, betting the U.S. can suffer longer than Iran can.

Larry Johnson: Thatâ€™s pure magical thinking. Look at the case studies: Cuba (66 years of sanctions â€” didnâ€™t work), North Korea, Russia â€” none of them surrendered. Iran has options: access to the Caspian Sea and Russia to the north, Turkmenistan, Pakistan, etc. Weâ€™re not sealing them off. This ignores reality.

Daniel Davis: The wild card in all of this is Israel. What role will they play?

Larry Johnson: Israel will try to destroy any prospect of an agreement if they can. Their words donâ€™t match their capabilities. Theyâ€™ve destroyed buildings in Gaza but after nearly three years still havenâ€™t defeated Hamas. Theyâ€™re bogged down in southern Lebanon fighting over towns like Bint Jbeil and taking significant casualties from Hezbollah.
The ceasefire in Lebanon was largely a cynical move to use the Lebanese army against Hezbollah. Israel will never accept Hezbollah as anything other than a terrorist group. Theyâ€™re trying to build on a cracked foundation.

Daniel Davis: Iran has made its position very clear. This isnâ€™t a real-estate negotiation where you can haggle. They want sanctions lifted, U.S. military out of the Gulf, reparations, and an end to attacks on Hezbollah and Lebanon. Itâ€™s black or white.

Larry Johnson: Exactly. Iran is the Islamic Republic of Iran. They have deep religious faith and a history of enduring pain. They will not surrender. They have alternative routes for oil and trade. Theyâ€™ve also shown the world they can disrupt the Strait of Hormuz, making themselves a player that must be taken seriously. Countries like Italy and Spain are already distancing themselves from U.S. and Israeli policy.

Daniel Davis: What about our allies in the Gulf States? Theyâ€™re losing massive amounts of money every day this stays closed. At what point do they pressure Trump to go back to Option 1 and get the Strait fully open?

Larry Johnson: I think that pressure is coming. Russia and China are actively courting the Gulf states, telling them they have alternatives and donâ€™t have to keep taking the abuse. The Gulf Arabs are like an abused spouse being offered counseling. The UAE may be too far gone, but the Saudis are starting to reconsider. That may be one of the few things that could move the needle away from escalation.

Daniel Davis: Weâ€™ll find out soon enough. Larry, really appreciate you making time on such a busy day. Thanks for coming on.
Larry Johnson: All right, my brother. Weâ€™ll see you later
